## Retry recommendation and scope boundary

**Do not start an unchanged-source retry solely on the currently available evidence.**
This was a complete valid selected staging observation and an actual numeric miss,
not an interrupted attempt that needs recovery. A second chance aimed only at
getting TBT below200 would select a favorable observation without diagnosing the
existing tail; the fact that only one metric failed or the excess is small is not
sufficient justification.

Relevant current rules are explicit:

- CODE-STYLE CS07: “Do not raise budgets or repeat full matrices to chase one number.”
- SITE-RELEASE-GATES measured-speed policy: investigate/calibrate variance under the
  same environment; do not repeatedly rerun until one passes or raise thresholds;
  retain every retry/result.
- SITE-STAGING operation specifies the supported retry **mechanism**: a new owner
  `/stage` or Re-run all jobs; never partial failed-jobs retry or mixed attempts.
  It does not establish a causal reason to discard a valid failed sample.
- The separate issue39 no-retry five-trial diagnostic protocol is not silently
  imported as this staging profile; it reinforces retained all-observation semantics,
  but this recommendation does not claim that historical protocol governs T03.

Recommended next decision: retain stage39923213843 FAIL and T03.5 open; present a
bounded performance prerequisite/diagnostic treatment under the same owning issue,
with actual current failure and unchanged-runtime proof. A diagnostic can use
retained traces first; any additional repeat/calibration must have a prespecified
question, bound and interpretation, not serve as an admission retry by default.
If evidence later identifies a concrete transient collection/infrastructure fault,
one complete unchanged-source retry may be reconsidered under the existing route,
retaining original failure and fresh head/main/predecessor checks. No such fault
has been established here. If a runtime, gate, runner or budget correction is
needed, that expands the presently approved selector-only implementation scope
and requires the maintainer's concrete scope decision, with all current limits
preserved until then. Passing source/RI/owning/preflight cannot supply hosted PASS.

No production, stable promotion, merge or parent AC completion is authorized by
this review. All whole AC01–AC08 remain open. This execution changed no repository
or GitHub state; only local raw-data inspection files, a boundary summary and this
receipt were written.

## Adequacy and bounded implementation guidance

D01–D04 remain necessary, feasible and observable. Reuse the existing writing-current job and tests/cause-probe.test.cjs; no duplicate production helper/runner, new dependency or broader framework is needed. Current toolchain lock already contains yaml2.9.1 transitively and js-yaml. Independently loaded yaml2.9.1 through the existing common.cjs toolRequire and parsed the actual workflow: root `on` and `jobs['writing-current'].if` are preserved correctly. Also independently loaded existing espree11.2.0 and parsed that actual condition as one ExpressionStatement with LogicalExpression root. This is parser availability/shape, not a routing evaluation. A new package/lock change is unnecessary.

The proposed concrete test-only approach reuses the existing locked eslint/espree dependency to parse the actual YAML condition, then interprets only whitelisted Literal, approved github MemberExpression, ==, && and || AST nodes. This is proportionate and avoids a new tokenizer/framework, eval/new Function or separately copied routing predicate. Require exactly one ExpressionStatement and validate the ENTIRE AST before short-circuit evaluation, so false branches cannot hide unsupported calls/operators/properties; reject extra statements and trailing syntax. Grouping is represented by the parsed AST. Include meaningful unsupported-node/operator/property and malformed-input negatives. Missing approved event properties may use the documented absent-property empty-string behavior rather than throw before testing rejection. The actual current expression is JS-compatible and needs no GitHub string-escape translation; the test harness is a bounded subset, not a full Actions expression implementation.

Use independent event truth-table expectations against the actual YAML-parsed condition, together with actual workflow trigger validation. Admit same-repository exact-label PR128 only with both owner sender and owner actor. Reject wrong PR, label, repository, actor, sender, missing identity and non-PR events. Preserve same-repository exact-label PR40's original behavior, including its absence of the new PR128-only owner guard. Verify pull_request trigger remains labeled-only; the job if condition alone cannot model a workflow trigger or distinguish synchronize when that event is never subscribed.

Do not inadvertently replace GitHub comparison semantics with JavaScript strict equality. Official GitHub Expressions documentation states that string equality ignores case and equality can coerce unlike types numerically: https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/expressions . I checked that primary source during this preflight. Model the relevant scalar subset explicitly and test it, or constrain and state the fixture type assumptions clearly; do not claim a complete GitHub Actions evaluator. Current actual PR numbers are numeric and mandatory repository identity is nonempty, while absent sender data must not authorize PR128. Exact-label wording describes the specified label expression, not a newly invented case-sensitive policy.

The interpreter is test-only in the existing owner, uses no eval/new Function and adds no dependency/production helper. It requires one ExpressionStatement, rejects comments and statement delimiters, then validates the entire AST before evaluating either branch. Whitelisted context properties are finite; unsupported nodes, operators, computed properties and literal syntax fail. Actual inactive-branch call negatives demonstrate that short-circuiting cannot hide executable syntax. Missing approved event fields become empty strings; all routing comparisons relevant to the current expression operate on bounded primitive fields. This is correctly labeled a JS-compatible Actions subset, not a universal Actions evaluator.

The expected event results are explicit external oracles derived from D01, not the workflow's own result. Actual trigger extraction prevents a correct job condition from falsely passing an ordinary-update journey. Existing helper tests independently establish trial-count/failure retention; new tests do not replace real native acquisition. Current tests include genuine bypass-sensitive sender/actor/fork rejection, historical compatibility and malformed interpreter input. No new requirement or changed oracle is needed before CI.

## Independent causal challenge and proposed treatment

**Finding NL02 — repeated source-linked layout flush, appropriate for a bounded proposal, not sole-cause proof.** I independently inspected all six diagnostic traces AND the original failed hosted Writing trace. Every one contains82 UpdateLayoutTree and82 Layout events with beginData.stackTrace naming armReading at generated navigation.js line750, the viewport-height expression mapped to canonical site/engine/scroll-entry.cjs751. Direct native script inspection confirms the location and surrounding reconcile call; these payload source positions must not be confused with unrelated CPU-profile location numbering.

| Trace | armReading style events / raw ms | armReading layout events / raw ms |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Original failed hosted |82 /13.816|82 /9.043|
| Ordinary1 |82 /13.297|82 /8.770|
| Ordinary2 |82 /15.724|82 /9.954|
| Ordinary3 |82 /13.799|82 /9.055|
| Ordinary4 |82 /13.030|82 /8.774|
| Ordinary5 |82 /13.037|82 /8.537|
| Separate sampled6 |82 /16.622|82 /10.844|

The actual source batches element rectangles in reconcile but then calls armReading for each unit. That function reads window.innerHeight/window.scrollY while previous units can have wrapped ink, attached separator layers and changed state attributes. The trace-linked read-after-write pattern is concrete. The same count appears in favorable ordinary samples and the overrun, so it cannot by itself explain the observed TBT range or identify the entire original213.5ms/ordinary245ms cause.

Independently reconstructed CPU-profile groups by pid/tid/profile-id, matched sample IDs to node call frames and restricted counts to target `/color/runtime/` scripts. The draft's armReading441, attachSeparators83, drawLineRun177, setPaintState147, paintShapes110 and lifecycle draw158 self-sample counts match. Counts are not milliseconds or causal counterfactuals. Browser/WebUI processes must remain separate; largest aggregate trace tasks cannot automatically be assigned to the page. Raw style/layout durations are not Lighthouse simulated TBT savings and must not be subtracted from those estimates.

T03-R as written is a defensible next proposal: snapshot geometry/focus after necessary cleanup at the native measurement boundary and before per-unit mutations; pass a bounded batch context through reconcile and enter callers. That is smaller and more source-grounded than changing Canvas or increasing budgets. Preserve focus, below-fold/bottom boundaries, visibility, re-entry/reverse, cancellation and existing route/six-active behavior.

Two practical limits must remain explicit in future requirements/testing:

1. Cleanup legitimately writes before a new geometry phase. The useful regression asserts no repeated geometry getter/read in the subsequent per-unit arming phase; a blanket “no reads after any write in reconcile” would reject necessary cleanup/measurement rather than test the intended invariant.
2. enter's re-arming path also has visibility/cleanup interactions, and caching focus/scroll values changes when they are observed. Verify those semantics at the batch boundary, including scroll anchoring; do not silently cache them across events. Other layout-sensitive paths (e.g. drainReading) are not automatically repaired by this narrow change and need no unsupported global-zero-flush claim.

A behavioral getter/write-order fixture in the existing owner, with an independent original-order fault detection, is suitable to prove the structural invariant. Only actual exact-source staging-edition validation with unchanged budgets can establish a performance result. No improvement magnitude or200ms pass is promised. No runtime treatment was authored or tested by this reviewer.

## Recommended next bounded treatment, not implemented or approved here

The evidence supports presenting a precise next treatment in canonical
scroll-entry.cjs: keep viewport height, scrollY and focused element as one immutable
batch geometry/focus snapshot, passed through armReading from BOTH reconcile and
IntersectionObserver enter callers. Remove live layout-dependent reads from the
per-unit write phase; do not disable content flight, separators or Canvas to obtain
a favorable number. Snapshot at the consistent native-geometry boundary—after any
cleanup that can change layout/scroll anchoring, alongside nativeRects/pageBottom,
before per-unit mutations. The exact implementation still requires requirements/
code review; stale global caching across resize, theme, scroll or navigation is
not the proposed invariant.

DOM writes; visible content/focused descendants remain stable; end-of-page safety,
upward exit/rearm/downward entry, pending six-active bound, generation/cancellation,
Off/reduced/no-Canvas and route separator/paper semantics are preserved. Regression
must detect a reintroduced live read after the first write, not assert variable
names. Existing source tests and an actual comparable staged edition remain the
acceptance route under original budgets. A narrow fix is a plausible experiment,
not permission to skip future hosted validation or promise enough TBT savings.

## Important cleanup-boundary guidance

Current reconcile captures an unused-for-arming scrollY before releaseReadingStates, then measures pageBottom/native rectangles/separators; armReading subsequently reads live viewport/scroll/focus in its mutation loop. The approved fix must place the relevant batch context with the post-cleanup native measurements, not simply hoist armReading's reads above releaseReadingStates. Use cleanup-induced scroll anchoring/focus changes in a fixture to distinguish those placements, and use the correct post-cleanup context consistently for previousY where applicable.

Current enter obtains pageBottom before its loop but performs clearState and getClientRects within the per-entry rearm branch. Merely caching viewport/focus once at callback entry would not prove the approved after-cleanup boundary. Audit that rearm path separately: distinguish necessary cleanup from arming writes, retain observed native entry semantics, and batch post-cleanup reads before rearming candidates. Do not accidentally move an outcome-changing cleanup across eligibility/active-state decisions without evidence. The completed candidate must establish the actual design chosen here; this guidance is not authored implementation.

A useful test has at least two eligible units so the first unit's mutation precedes the old second-unit read; a single-unit counter cannot expose interleaving. Log controlled cleanup, geometry/focus reads and arming writes with phase boundaries. A cleanup mutation may change scroll/focus, while geometry mock getters themselves may read window.scrollY: count/classify those as pre-write measurements rather than confusing them with armReading reads. For enter, construct/retain IntersectionObserver entries before starting the callback phase so harness-side rectangle construction is not misreported as controller work. Exercise a second batch with changed viewport/scroll/focus to reject stale caching.

Independent eligibility oracles should preserve exact boundary comparisons: native.top equal to viewport height remains eligible unless another guard excludes it; native.bottom + scroll offset + reading distance +32 equal to pageBottom is excluded. Focus means a focused descendant belongs to its containing unit, not only direct element equality. Keep hidden content native, and test multiple rearm candidates so cleanup and shared ancestor separator ownership cannot hide order defects. Reuse existing repeated-entry, dense-six-active, cancellation/print/focus/readiness and unsupported-motion tests rather than duplicate their matrices.

### REQ-R01 — Native shape missing in forward-preservation fixture

Severity: blocking test-oracle defect for current R02 candidate readiness; bounded correction, no new scope or approval needed.

Source: tests/scroll-entry.test.cjs, new `forward mixed observer entries preserve queue order while an active shell exits` test, callback entries for newly intersecting7 and exiting1.

Both hand-built entries omit boundingClientRect. Original valid runtime evaluates `entry.boundingClientRect.top >= window.innerHeight && direction < 0` for a nonintersecting entry; because the property is absent, it throws before direction short-circuiting and the guarded callback settles state. Thus the original source's failure of this new alleged preservation test does not demonstrate a changed queue order. Real IntersectionObserver entries always contain their native rectangle; the fixture must supply it. The corrected runtime avoids the access on forward direction, so the malformed test happens to pass42/42 today, concealing this oracle issue.

Treatment: supply valid independent native rectangles for the modeled callback entries while preserving their order and desired active/queued state expectations. The forward preservation control should pass the original source, whereas the two newly added geometry/focus regressions should fail it. This is an ordinary proposed correction, not authored repository test code by this reviewer. Recheck only affected fixture/negative scope plus relevant suite; unchanged runtime/evidence can be reused.

## Independently derived risks and oracles

The required invariant is an event-local coherent native state after layout-affecting cleanup, followed by preparation writes. Caching earlier or across events is insufficient. Existing conditions imply native/top equality remains eligible, bottom equality remains excluded, hidden/focused content remains readable, upward motion remains native, and forward queue order cannot change merely to simplify batching.

| Risk / condition | Independent expectation and selected check | Actual result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Per-unit viewport/focus reads follow a previous unit's writes, in reconcile or upward enter | At least two eligible units must be armed; instrumented viewport/scroll/focus/extent/native-box reads precede the first waiting-state write. Original runtime and a live-height reread should fail. | Both new candidate ordering tests PASS; original runtime fails both; live-height mutant fails both on explicit read-after-write assertion. |
| Cleanup changes height, native scroll anchoring, focus or page extent | Snapshot must follow cleanup, with new values on a second batch. Early snapshots and early cached focus must fail independently of getter-count assertions. | Each early-snapshot and each focus-only early-cache mutant fails the independent expected final states. Candidate two-event fixtures PASS. |
| Cached observer rectangles survive cleanup | Observer data identifies a full exit, but actual arming eligibility uses fresh native boxes/visibility after the complete upward cleanup batch. | Candidate upward fixture passes with deliberately stale delivered rectangles; stale-observer-rect mutant fails the fresh native rectangle assertion. |
| Fold / bottom / hidden / focused-descendant boundaries | With height700, scroll100: native top700 is allowed. Absolute bottom1700 + distance88 + guard32 equals pageBottom1820, so it stays native. Hidden unit stays native; a containing unit of the focused descendant stays native. New event changes which units qualify. | Candidate states match both explicit vectors. Changing `<` to `<=` at the fold or `>=` to `>` at the bottom each fails. Focus-only mutants fail in both callers. |
| Overeager batching changes forward mixed-entry ordering | First incoming crossing sees six occupied slots and stays queued, even if a later callback entry releases one; a subsequent valid completion drains it. | Corrected control passes candidate AND original runtime. Moving cleanup ahead of forward processing fails: incoming shell is entering rather than waiting. |
| Generation/cancellation/reverse/native jumps/readiness/Off/unsupported paths or six-active bounds regress | Retain native readable content, bounded timers/listeners, repeatable full exits, current-generation events only, six active slots and ordinary queue draining. | Existing relevant cases PASS within the 62-test execution; relevant branches/source unchanged. |
| Separators/route phases and DOM wrapping acquire hidden measurement after arming begins | `wrapInk`/`attachSeparators` perform writes without native geometry reads; separator models are measured before per-unit writes in reconciliation. Shared separator ownership and route cleanup remain intact. | Inspected actual callees; existing separator, route, cancellation and native-row tests PASS. No browser layout measurement claimed. |

The first reconciliation event's expected waiting units are indices1,4,7; index0 is visible, index2 contains focus, index3 hidden, indices5/6 violate bottom space. The next event's waiting units are2,5,6,7, using the changed viewport/scroll/focus/extent. Upward entries1–4 expect waiting/waiting/native/waiting in the focused first batch, then native/waiting/waiting/waiting after changed native geometry and cleared focus. These expectations follow visibility/overflow/focus semantics, not the helper's implementation text.

The initial pre-cleanup `height` in enter only classifies delivered full exits. The separate post-cleanup snapshot drives arming. Forward entries retain their original in-order outcome decisions; only upward cleanup is batched. `previousY` now uses the same post-cleanup scroll snapshot as reconciliation eligibility. No persistent geometry/focus cache was introduced.
